### Activity 1 – 14: Discounts and increases

1. The price of a tub of margarine is $\text{R } 6{,}99$. If the price rises by $10\%$, how much will it cost?

---

**Solution:**

New price is $\text{R } 6{,}99 + 10\% \text{ of R } 6{,}99 = \text{R } 6{,}99 + 70\text{ c (rounded off)} = \text{R } 7{,}69$  
OR New price is $(100 + 10)\% \text{ of R } 6{,}99 = 110\% \text{ of R } 6{,}99 = \frac{110}{100} \times \frac{6{,}99}{1} = \text{R } 7{,}69\text{ (rounded off)}$

---

### Question 2

Top Teenage T-shirts have a $20\%$ discount on all T-shirts. If one of their T-shirts originally cost $\text{R } 189{,}90$, what will you pay for it now?

**Solution:**

You only pay $80\%$ $(100\% - 20\% \text{ discount})$. Thus: 

$$\frac{80}{100} \times 189{,}90 = \text{R } 151{,}92$$

OR 

$$20\% \text{ of R } 189{,}90 = \frac{20}{100} \times 189{,}90$$

The discount is thus $\text{R } 37{,}98$. You pay:

$$\text{R } 189{,}90 - \text{R } 37{,}98 = \text{R } 151{,}92$$

d) $12\ 345;\ 120\ 345;\ 120,54;\ 542\ 120;\ 55\ 420$  

#### Solution:
a) $6\ 650\ 232;\ 5\ 355\ 005;\ 3\ 695\ 212;\ 635\ 765;\ 365\ 280;$  
b) $7812;\ 1278;\ 872;\ 78,2;\ 28,27;\ 27,28;$  
c) $9988;\ 8903;\ 3989;\ 893;\ 89,89;\ 89,30$  
d) $542\ 120;\ 120\ 345;\ 55\ 420;\ 12\ 345;\ 120,54$

### Exercise

2. Rewrite the following calculations with brackets to make the answers correct:
   a) $23 + 6 \times 5 = 145$  
   b) $12 + 2 \times 82 = 176$  
   c) $18 + 3 \times 17 = 69$  
   d) $18 + 3 \times 17 = 357$  
   e) $15 + 7 \times 5 = 110$  
   f) $65 \times 2 + 5 = 455$  
   g) $115 + 4 \times 12 = 163$  

#### Solution:
a) $(23 + 6) \times 5 = 145$  
b) $12 + (2 \times 82) = 176$ OR $12 + 2 \times 82 = 176$ (brackets not needed)  
c) $18 + (3 \times 17) = 69$ OR $18 + 3 \times 17 = 69$ (brackets not needed)  
d) $(18 + 3) \times 17 = 357$  
e) $(15 + 7) \times 5 = 110$  
f) $65 \times (2 + 5) = 455$  
g) $115 + (4 \times 12) = 163$ OR $115 + 4 \times 12 = 163$ (brackets not needed)  

---

3. For each of the following questions, calculate the answer and then round it up or down depending on the situation. Explain why you rounded the way you did.

   a) Liam is packing mushrooms. There are $15$ mushrooms in a punnet. He has $275$ mushrooms to pack. How many punnets does he need?  
   
   b) A car mechanic charges $\text{R }200$ per hour or part thereof for labour. How much would he charge for a job that takes $270\text{ minutes}$?  
   
   c) An amusement park ride has places for $30$ people. $82$ people are in the queue for the ride. How many times will the ride need to run?  
   
   d) Nokuthula is putting up washing lines in her yard. The distance from one pole to the other is $3,2\text{ m}$. How many lines can she put up if she has $18\text{ m}$ of washing line?  

#### Solution:
a) $275 \div 15 = 18,33\dots$ He can only sell full punnets, so he must round down to $18$.

### **Question 4**

How much would a customer pay for each of these totals in a shop?

a) $\text{R } 215{,}67$  
b) $\text{R } 329{,}29$  
c) $\text{R } 65{,}33$  

#### **Solution:**
a) $\text{R } 215{,}65$  
b) $\text{R } 329{,}25$  
c) $\text{R } 65{,}30$

### **Question 5**

For each of the following problems, first write two ratios equal to each other, then find the unknown value:

a) A recipe calls for $\frac{1}{3}\text{ cup}$ sugar to $2\text{ cups}$ of flour. How many cups of flour do you need to add to $3\text{ cups}$ of sugar?  
b) A survey shows that $5 : 1$ learners in a school have their own cell phone. If there are $1350\text{ learners}$ in the school, how many do not have their own cell phone?  

#### **Solution:**
a) $\frac{1}{3} : 2 = 1 : 6 = 3 : 18$. So you need to add $18\text{ cups}$ of flour to $3\text{ cups}$ of sugar.  
b) There are $6\text{ parts}$ in the ratio, so $1\text{ part} = 1350 \div 6 = 225\text{ learners}$ do not have their own cell phone.  

---

### **Question 6**

Three litres of milk cost $\text{R } 29{,}95$ at Shop A and two litres of milk cost $\text{R } 15{,}95$ at Shop B.

a) What is the cost per litre at each shop?  
b) Which is the better buy?  
c) How much would five litres of milk cost at each shop?  

#### **Solution:**
a) **Shop A:** $\text{R } 29{,}95 \div 3 = \text{R } 9{,}98$. **Shop B:** $\text{R } 15{,}95 \div 2 = \text{R } 7{,}98$  
b) **Shop B.**  
c) **Shop A:** $\text{R } 9{,}98 \times 5 = \text{R } 49{,}90$. **Shop B:** $\text{R } 7{,}98 \times 5 = \text{R } 39{,}90$

### **Question 7**

Two different sized jars of jam are sold at the following prices: A: $500\text{ g}$ for $\text{R } 8{,}50$ or B: $750\text{ g}$ for $\text{R } 11{,}50$. Which size is the better buy?

---

**Solution:**  
Work out what $100\text{ g}$ costs for each. $\text{A: R } 8{,}50 \div 5 = \text{R } 1{,}70$ and $\text{B: R } 11{,}50 \div 7{,}5 = \text{R } 1{,}533\dots$ So jar B is cheaper.

10. The price of a new car is $\text{R } 210\ 000$. Mr Simelane is offered a $12\%$ discount. How much will he pay?

   **Solution:**  
   $\text{R } 210\ 000 - 12\% = \text{R } 184\ 800$

---

11. A packet of rice weighs $1{,}5\text{ kg}$ when it is bought. Some of the rice has been used and the packet now weighs $15\%$ less. What is the weight of the rice that was used?

---

**Solution:**
$1,5\text{ kg} - 15\% = 1,275\text{ kg}$. $1,5\text{ kg} - 1,275\text{ kg} = 0,225\text{ kg}$ of rice was used.

---

12. The price of a TV set is $\text{R } 2786$. If a buyer is offered an $11\%$ discount, what does he pay for it?

    **Solution:**
    $$\text{R } 2786 - 11\% = \text{R } 2479,54$$

---

13. A car was valued at $\text{R } 175\text{ }000$ when it was purchased. After three years it was sold for $\text{R } 82\text{ }000$. What percentage of its original value did the car lose?

    **Solution:**
    $$\text{R } 175\text{ }000 - \text{R } 82\text{ }000 = \text{R } 93\text{ }000$$
    $$\frac{93\text{ }000}{175\text{ }000}\% = 53,14\%$$
